## First-pass formalization order
1. Review the extracted candidates against the PDF text.
2. Separate deterministic algebra from probability, asymptotic, and distributional statements.
3. Formalize the deterministic algebra first when it can reuse earlier chapter infrastructure.
4. Add the needed measure/probability infrastructure before attempting the main stochastic theorem.

  Definition 10.2-to-quantile wrappers, finite-measure CDF monotonicity wrappers, law-CDF
  specializations for scalar probability laws, a.e.-measurability/probability wrappers that
  discharge the lower-quantile CDF bracketing premises at levels `0 < p < 1`, Definition
  10.2-facing lower-quantile coverage/test wrappers, theorem-facing probability-level
  coverage/test wrappers that discharge those quantile premises from probability conditional laws,
  pointwise a.e.-measurability, and `0 < α < 1`, non-strict local-limit-CDF bracket wrappers for
  percentile, percentile-`t`, and bootstrap critical-value quantiles, concrete lower generalized inverses, a
  Stieltjes-function wrapper that discharges the right-continuity bracketing premise, and a
  percentile endpoint constructor that converts scaled bootstrap lower quantiles back to
  original-scale interval endpoints. The percentile endpoint route now also has indexed
  sample-size-dependent bootstrap-distribution wrappers for ordinary nonparametric bootstrap laws,
  including local-limit-CDF and strict-CDF probability-level forms, plus fixed/indexed law-facing
  strict-CDF and local-limit-CDF wrappers where a `HasLaw` premise identifies an auxiliary scalar
  bootstrap limit's CDF.
- Theorem 10.14's percentile-`t` coverage bridge is formalized: positive standard errors turn
  Hansen's interval into the event `q*_{α/2} <= T <= q*_{1-α/2}`, and joint convergence gives
  the limiting coverage probability, with a scalar-event calibrated wrapper for `qL <= ξ <= qU`.
  Scalar endpoint boundary-null assumptions now supply the vector null-frontier premise, and
  non-atomic scalar limit laws can supply both the boundary-null and interval-mass calibration.
  Componentwise Slutsky plus endpoint-CDF wrappers now state the `1−α` conclusion directly from
  t-ratio convergence and endpoint convergence in probability, including the symmetric endpoint
  specialization with limiting bootstrap endpoints `-q` and `q`; a lower-generalized-inverse
  constructor identifies those endpoints from pointwise conditional bootstrap CDF convergence, and
  its probability-level Definition 10.2 wrapper discharges the quantile bracketing premises. The
  percentile-`t` route also has indexed sample-size-dependent bootstrap-distribution wrappers for
  ordinary nonparametric bootstrap laws, including local-limit-CDF and strict-CDF probability-level
  forms, plus fixed/indexed law-facing strict-CDF and local-limit-CDF wrappers where a `HasLaw`
  premise identifies an auxiliary scalar bootstrap t-ratio limit's CDF. Fixed and indexed
  regression-facing wrappers now feed the Theorem 10.18 studentized transformed-statistic route
  directly into this percentile-`t` coverage theorem, with strict-CDF and local standard-normal CDF
  bracketing variants; fixed/indexed HC0-HC3 OLS wrappers now specialize the actual sample interval
  in both strict-CDF and local-CDF-bracketing forms while discharging the ordinary sample t-ratio
  convergence premise from Chapter 7.
- Theorem 10.16's two-sided bootstrap-test bridge is formalized: joint convergence of the test
  statistic and bootstrap critical value implies convergence of rejection probabilities, with a
  calibrated size-`α` wrapper and a scalar-event form for `q < |ξ|`. Scalar critical-value
  boundary-null assumptions now supply the vector null-frontier premise, and non-atomic scalar
  limit laws can supply both the boundary-null and rejection-mass calibration. Componentwise
  Slutsky plus endpoint-CDF wrappers now state the size-`α` conclusion directly from statistic
  convergence and critical-value convergence in probability, including a lower-generalized-inverse
  constructor, local-limit-CDF bracket variants, probability-level Definition 10.2 wrappers for
  bootstrap critical values, and indexed sample-size-dependent bootstrap-distribution wrappers with
  local-limit-CDF and strict-CDF probability-level forms, plus fixed/indexed law-facing strict-CDF
  and local-limit-CDF wrappers where a `HasLaw` premise identifies an auxiliary
  absolute-statistic limit's CDF. Fixed and indexed regression-facing
  wrappers now feed Theorem 10.18's absolute studentized bootstrap t-statistic CDF route directly
  into this two-sided bootstrap-test theorem, and fixed/indexed HC0-HC3 OLS wrappers now discharge
  the ordinary sample t-statistic convergence premise from Chapter 7.
- Theorems 10.15 and 10.17 now have second-order transfer wrappers: once the bootstrap quantile or
  critical-value argument supplies an `o(n⁻¹)` replacement error relative to the fixed symmetric
  interval/test probability, the fixed-critical Edgeworth expansion transfers to the random
  percentile-`t` interval or bootstrap test. Event-specialized wrappers state these transfers for
  the actual percentile-`t` interval event and two-sided bootstrap-test rejection event, with
  polynomial Edgeworth-shape counterparts.
- The Marcinkiewicz WLLN in Theorem 10.20 is formalized in its real-exponent `r > 1`
  form, including the deterministic max-times-mean inequality used in Hansen's proof and the
  uniformly-integrable wrapper. The ordinary-bootstrap shifted `Fin (n+1)` form is available for
  both the real-exponent theorem and the natural-power convenience face used by integer-moment
  applications.
- The concrete ordinary nonparametric-bootstrap CLT route is now formalized through the indexed
  weak/CDF, scalar projection, characteristic-function, covariance, and iid covariance-tail
  wrappers, and its scalar conditional-variance route is now formalized through empirical variance,
  iid, and `iIndepFun` constructors. Scalar ordinary-bootstrap percentile, percentile-t, and
  absolute-test constructors now feed the concrete `Fin (n+1)` resample-mean CLT into
  Theorems 10.13, 10.14, and 10.16 with strict-CDF and local-CDF bracketing. Concrete
  nonparametric-bootstrap constructors remain to be added for the refinement, regression, and
  further model-specific percentile/test results. Theorem 10.2's
  scalar and vector conditional-Chebyshev/Markov constructors are formalized, including
  sample-size-indexed ordinary nonparametric-bootstrap scalar and Euclidean-vector WLLNs on
  `Fin (n+1) -> Fin (n+1)` resampling spaces, iid-integrable scalar and Euclidean-vector level
  wrappers, finite empirical resample-mean, coordinate-marginal, scalar/vector sample-mean
  variance and covariance, trace, Euclidean norm second-moment, and squared-norm identities for
  the one-draw calculation.

## LaTeX / Lean Crosswalk

This file is a chapter-level crosswalk between textbook statements and Lean formalizations.

Conventions:
- All links in this file are relative.
- At this stage the middle column is a compact theorem-surface summary, not necessarily a polished LaTeX rendering.
- Leave the Lean column blank unless the repo already contains a real theorem.
- Use this file for statement-level mapping; this content is now part of the canonical chapter inventory.

limit.<br>[chapter10_bootstrap_smooth_variance_consistency_of_deterministic_plugins](../HansenEconometrics/Chapter10Bootstrap.lean) covers deterministic continuous plug-ins: ordinary convergence in `V(U_n)`.<br>[chapter10_bootstrap_smooth_variance_consistency_of_deterministic_jacobian](../HansenEconometrics/Chapter10Bootstrap.lean) covers a deterministic Jacobian `G(U_n)` with a separately plug-in components: ordinary convergence in probability of `G_n` and `V_n` is lifted through Theorem 10.1 and then fed through the smooth covariance
